  - issue statement: Critical-value notification is represented as a boolean while recipient, time, acknowledgement, and authority evidence remain outside the ontology. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification model Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:100-106; .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11 Source finding context: Critical-value notification is reduced to a boolean while the actual recipient and time authority are external, weakening the ontology's operational-risk purpose. Source finding context: For an EMR/LIS integration concept authority, critical-value notification is a stakeholder- and safety-critical workflow, not a peripheral annotation. A boolean cannot represent who was notified, when, by whom, by which channel, or whether acknowledgement closed the loop. By pushing the meaningful notification evidence into an unmodeled ledger, the ontology accepts an implicit tradeoff against traceability and integration clarity without justifying it. Source finding context: Promote critical-value notification into an explicit concept such as `CriticalValueNotification` with result/test reference, recipient, notifier, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement status, and source authority. Relate it to `CriticalValue`, `Result`, `Staff`, and the EMR/LIS release workflow. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration while identifying operational risks in entities, relations, and states. Source finding context: A critical value is reported or audited and the integration needs to determine notification timing, recipient, acknowledgement, or authoritative source from the ontology. Source finding context: The ontology cannot support the clinically important closed-loop notification semantics it gestures at, so downstream systems may treat a bare boolean as sufficient evidence of completed risk handling. Source finding context: The model treats critical-value communication evidence as external operational bookkeeping instead of a first-class integration concept. Source finding context: `CriticalValue` represents immediately notifiable dangerous result ranges. Source finding context: The only notification attribute is `notified: boolean`; time and recipient are said to exist in a phone log ledger outside the modeled ontology. Source finding context: The review purpose is suitability as an EMR/LIS integration authority with operational-risk focus.

  - failure condition: A critical value is reported or audited and the integration needs notification timing, recipient, acknowledgement, or authoritative source semantics. Source finding context: A critical value is reported or audited and the integration needs to determine notification timing, recipient, acknowledgement, or authoritative source from the ontology.
  - impact: Downstream systems may treat a bare boolean as sufficient evidence of completed risk handling, weakening auditability and clinical communication traceability. Source finding context: The ontology cannot support the clinically important closed-loop notification semantics it gestures at, so downstream systems may treat a bare boolean as sufficient evidence of completed risk handling.
  - root hypothesis: The model treats critical-value communication evidence as external operational bookkeeping instead of a first-class integration concept.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md:100-106`, `review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:100-106; .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-002`, `finding-002.cause-001`, `materialized-input.md:100-101`, `finding-002.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md:102-106`, `finding-002.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-002.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose because critical values are operational-risk events that must support traceable clinical communication. If the ontology is the EMR/LIS concept authority, downstream systems should not have to infer closed-loop notification status from an external phone ledger or a boolean that cannot show who was notified, when, by whom, through what channel, or whether acknowledgement closed the loop.
  - root cause: The starting point is that critical-value communication evidence is treated as external bookkeeping instead of a first-class integration concept. Once the evidence remains outside the ontology, the modeled concept can only expose a lossy boolean and cannot carry the workflow semantics needed for audit, release, or risk handling.
  - causal path: The causal path starts with `CriticalValue` representing immediately notifiable dangerous result ranges, then shows that its only notification attribute is `notified: boolean` while recipient and time are left to an external phone log. Against the review purpose of using the ontology as an EMR/LIS integration authority with operational-risk focus, that modeling choice creates the observed gap: notification is gestured at but not represented with the actors, timing, acknowledgement, or authority evidence required by the workflow.
  - action: Promote critical-value notification into an explicit event or entity, such as `CriticalValueNotification`, linked to `CriticalValue`, `Result`, `Staff`, and the EMR/LIS release workflow. It should carry recipient, notifier, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement status, and source authority so systems can distinguish a dangerous result threshold from the communication event that closes or audits the clinical risk loop.

- issue-003 (medium): TAT is a material authority gap: it is named as an ontology concept, but the ontology neither governs it as a derived metric nor clearly declares the dashboard formula outside its authority boundary.

  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, structure, logic, semantics

  - issue statement: TAT is mentioned as an ontology concept but its calculation authority is left to an external dashboard formula without governed metric semantics. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / deferred local metric and catalog authorities Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:46-60,126-128; .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11 Source finding context: The ontology knowingly leaves catalog and TAT authority split across local teams, creating purpose drift for a document intended to be the integration concept authority. Leaving dual catalog registration and a separate dashboard-owned TAT formula as accepted current behavior privileges local operational convenience over canonical concept authority. The problem is not that future cleanup is planned; it is that the current authority document records the split without a governing precedence rule, drift-detection obligation, or ownership boundary. Source finding context: Add explicit authority and precedence rules: define whether `Test` or `Assay` owns orderable versus executable catalog identity, how dual registration is reconciled, and which source owns TAT semantics. If the dashboard formula remains separate, model it as a derived projection with required conformance to the ontology's canonical TAT definition. Source finding context: A new test/assay or TAT-consuming integration is implemented while catalog identity or TAT semantics differ between the ontology and local dashboard/catalog maintenance practices. Source finding context: Downstream consumers can implement conflicting meanings while still appearing to follow the authority document, reducing trust in the ontology as the integration source of truth. Source finding context: The ontology accepts unresolved local authority splits as current practice without encoding precedence, ownership, or reconciliation duties. Source finding context: `Test` is the orderable catalog unit, while `Assay` is the analyzer execution unit with overlapping department and specimen-kind semantics. Source finding context: The notes require new items to be registered in both places until future catalog cleanup. Source finding context: The notes define TAT from collection to release, but also say the dashboard team maintains its own calculation formula. Source finding context: The document is being reviewed for suitability as an EMR/LIS integration concept authority. Source finding context: TAT is an operational performance value spanning specimen collection and report release. Because the artifact positions itself as the EMR/LIS authority model, leaving a cross-system metric to an external team-owned formula creates a coverage gap for a time-dependent value that consumers are likely to compare or report. Source finding context: Model TAT as a governed derived metric with source timestamps, calculation owner, inclusion/exclusion rules, effective versioning, and behavior for amended/corrected reports; alternatively declare the dashboard metric explicitly out of scope with a canonical integration boundary. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: Use as a concept authority for EMR/LIS integration, including operational values derived across workflow timestamps. Source finding context: When EMR, LIS, or dashboard consumers need the canonical TAT definition or need to reproduce historical TAT after formula changes or report amendments, the ontology points to an external team-owned calculation rather than a governed concept. Source finding context: A key operational metric can diverge across consumers and cannot be reconstructed from the ontology's own authority model beyond the two raw timestamps. Source finding context: The ontology delegates TAT calculation authority to a dashboard-maintained formula instead of modeling TAT as a governed derived concept. Source finding context: The model includes collected_at on Specimen and released_at on Report. Source finding context: A key operational metric can diverge across consumers and cannot be reconstructed from the ontology's own authority model beyond the two raw timestamps.
  - root hypothesis: Operational metric authority is delegated to local dashboard practice without modeling ownership, versioning, precedence, or conformance to the ontology.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003`, `materialized-input.md:46-60`, `materialized-input.md:126-128`, `review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:46-60,126-128; .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:1-3`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:32-39`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:70-79`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:121-123`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:32-39, clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:70-79, clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:121-123`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-003`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-007`, `rel-013`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-013`, `finding-003.cause-001`, `finding-003.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md:126-127`, `finding-003.cause-003`, `materialized-input.md:128`, `finding-003.cause-004`, `finding-007.cause-001`, `finding-007.cause-002`, `finding-007.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:8-123`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-003.structure`, `Decision whether TAT is inside ontology authority as a governed derived metric or explicitly outside as a local dashboard/reporting metric.`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-008`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: axiology, coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's declared purpose as an EMR/LIS integration concept authority because consumers can treat TAT as authoritative while applying different dashboard, LIS, or reporting formulas. When formulas change or reports are amended, the ontology provides only source timestamps, not the governed metric semantics needed to reproduce or compare TAT consistently.
  - root cause: The starting point is the missing authority boundary or derived-metric contract for TAT. Because the ontology delegates calculation practice to a dashboard formula without defining ownership, versioning, exclusions, precedence, conformance, or amendment behavior, downstream divergence is permitted before any consumer implementation decision is made.
  - causal path: The ontology includes the relevant workflow timestamps, such as specimen collection and report release, and notes define TAT from those timestamps. The same notes also leave the calculation formula with the dashboard team, and no ontology concept captures the formula owner, effective period, exclusions, recalculation rules, or precedence. That path turns TAT from a shared integration concept into a locally interpreted operational metric.
  - action: Before the next stage, decide whether TAT is inside or outside ontology authority. If inside, model it as a governed derived metric with source timestamps, owner, version/effective rules, exclusions, conformance requirements, and amended/corrected report behavior. If outside, explicitly declare the dashboard metric out of scope and define the integration boundary so consumers do not mistake it for canonical ontology semantics.

- issue-004 (medium): Specimen lifecycle is materially incomplete because the shared ontology stops at analyzed and does not model or govern post-analysis custody, retention, retrieval, storage, or disposal states/events.

Source finding context: For an EMR/LIS integration concept authority, specimen traceability does not end at analysis. Retention, storage location, disposal, and exception states are operationally relevant after result production, and the target itself acknowledges the post-analyzed segment while leaving it outside the ontology. Source finding context: Add explicit post-analysis specimen lifecycle concepts such as retained/stored, retrieved, disposed, and disposal/retention events with timestamp, responsible department/staff, location, and policy basis, or define a governed extension point that is still visible in the authority model. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use as the concept authority document for EMR/LIS integration from order through report, including entity, relationship, and state modeling. Source finding context: When downstream systems need to reconcile specimen retention, retrieval, disposal, or post-analysis custody after analyzed, the authority model has no canonical states or events to map to. Source finding context: Integrations may treat analyzed as terminal even though the artifact states post-analysis handling exists, weakening traceability and making department-specific implementations the de facto authority. Source finding context: The target delegates post-analyzed specimen handling to departmental rules instead of modeling it as part of the shared ontology. Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle terminates at analyzed. Source finding context: The ontology nevertheless acknowledges storage/disposal after analyzed as an operational process. Source finding context: Because no shared post-analysis lifecycle concepts are defined, EMR/LIS integrations lack a canonical representation for specimen custody after analysis.

  - failure condition: Downstream systems need to reconcile specimen retention, retrieval, disposal, or post-analysis custody after analyzed.   - impact: Integrations may treat analyzed as terminal even though post-analysis handling exists, leaving department-specific implementations as the de facto authority. Source finding context: Integrations may treat analyzed as terminal even though the artifact states post-analysis handling exists, weakening traceability and making department-specific implementations the de facto authority.
  - root hypothesis: The target delegates post-analyzed specimen handling to departmental rules instead of modeling it as part of the shared ontology.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:1-3`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:32-39`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:116-119`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:32-39, clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:116-119`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-004`, `finding-004.cause-001`, `finding-004.cause-002`, `finding-004.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-004.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage

  - materiality: The declared purpose is to serve as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from order through report, including entity, relationship, and state modeling. When post-analysis handling must be reconciled, the model has no canonical states or events for downstream systems to map to, so integrations may treat analyzed as terminal and let department-specific rules become the effective authority.
  - root cause: The starting point is that post-analyzed specimen handling is delegated to departmental rules instead of being represented in the shared authority model. That delegation leaves the shared lifecycle without concepts for custody after analysis even though the operational process exists.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported by the lifecycle ending at analyzed, while the ontology acknowledges storage or disposal after analysis as an operational process. Because those post-analysis concepts are not included as shared lifecycle states or events, EMR/LIS integrations lack a canonical representation for retention, retrieval, disposal, storage location, responsible party, timing, and policy basis after analysis.
  - action: Add explicit post-analysis specimen lifecycle concepts such as retained/stored, retrieved, disposed, and retention/disposal events, including timestamp, responsible department or staff, location, and policy basis. If the detailed rules must remain departmental, add a governed extension point that is still visible in the authority model so downstream integrations have a canonical mapping target.

- issue-005 (medium): CriticalValue currently conflates the definition of a dangerous threshold/range with the state of notifying someone about a patient-specific critical result. This makes the ontology unable to cleanly represent either the threshold rule or the auditable notification event.
  - issue stance agreement: 6/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, logic, semantics, structure

  - issue statement: CriticalValue mixes dangerous range thresholds with notification state, so notification evidence and threshold rules are not cleanly separated. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification coverage Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-101 Source finding context: Critical-value notification is represented only as a boolean and excludes notification evidence concepts. Source finding context: Critical-value communication is a controlled action in the target domain because the entity is defined as requiring immediate notification. A boolean cannot reconstruct who notified whom, when, through what channel, and with what acknowledgement, so the authority model does not cover the audit dimension needed for operational trust. Source finding context: Replace or supplement notified with a CriticalValueNotification event/entity containing critical_value/result reference, notifier, recipient, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement/status, escalation fields, and evidence/source-log linkage. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use as an EMR/LIS integration concept authority for operationally risky lab workflow states and controlled actions. Source finding context: When a critical result requires proof of notification or reconciliation between LIS, EMR, and a phone log, the model exposes only a true/false flag and no auditable event structure. Source finding context: Systems can disagree or overwrite the notification flag without preserving the evidence needed to establish clinical communication history. Source finding context: The ontology keeps critical-value notification evidence outside the modeled concept set and collapses notification into a boolean. Source finding context: CriticalValue is defined as a result range requiring immediate notification. Source finding context: The only in-model notification attribute is notified: boolean, with time and recipient delegated to a phone log. Source finding context: The model therefore lacks the actor/time/recipient/evidence concepts needed to cover the controlled notification action inside the EMR/LIS authority model. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:CriticalValue Source finding context: CriticalValue가 위험 결과 값 범위와 통보 완료 상태를 한 개념에 섞어 threshold rule과 notification event의 존재론적 유형을 혼합한다. Source finding context: 위험 결과 값 범위는 검사별 판정 기준 또는 rule이고, 통보 여부는 특정 환자 결과에 대한 사건 상태입니다. 이 둘이 하나의 entity에 있으면 '이 범위가 통보되었는가'처럼 범위 자체의 상태로 읽히며, 환자별 critical result occurrence와 기준 정의가 의미적으로 뒤섞입니다. Source finding context: When a critical result requires proof of notification or reconciliation between LIS, EMR, and a phone log, the model exposes only a true/false flag and no auditable event structure. Source finding context: 검사별 위험 범위 정의에 환자별 통보 완료 여부를 저장하거나 해석할 때.
  - impact: A single notified flag can be read as either threshold state or patient-result event state, weakening auditability and semantic clarity. Source finding context: Systems can disagree or overwrite the notification flag without preserving the evidence needed to establish clinical communication history. Source finding context: 한 range 레코드의 notified 값이 특정 결과 사건인지 기준 자체인지 불명확해 critical value 통보 추적, 재통보, 감사 기록의 의미가 손상될 수 있습니다.
  - root hypothesis: The model fails to separate critical threshold definitions from patient/result-specific notification events.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:1-3`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-101`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-005`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-017`, `rel-006`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-006`, `finding-005.cause-001`, `finding-005.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:97-101`, `finding-005.cause-003`, `finding-017.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-96`, `finding-017.cause-002`, `finding-017.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:101`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.axiology`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-005`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-005.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: coverage, semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to serve as an EMR/LIS integration authority for operationally risky lab workflow states and controlled actions. Critical-value notification is such a controlled action: systems need to know not only that notification occurred, but who notified whom, when, through what channel, with what acknowledgement, and from what evidence source. A single notified boolean can be overwritten or interpreted inconsistently across LIS, EMR, and phone-log reconciliation, weakening auditability and semantic clarity for safety-relevant communication.
  - root cause: The starting point is that the model does not separate critical threshold definitions from patient/result-specific notification events. A threshold or range is a rule about when a result is dangerous; notification is an event or state tied to a particular result occurrence. Placing lower_bound, upper_bound, and notified on the same CriticalValue concept gives one concept two different authorities and timelines.
  - causal path: The causal path is: CriticalValue is defined as a dangerous result range requiring immediate notification; the same concept carries lower and upper bounds plus only a notified boolean; the detailed notification facts are left outside the modeled concept set, with time and recipient delegated to a separate phone log. That leaves no clean in-model event target for actor, recipient, time, channel, acknowledgement, escalation, or evidence linkage, so the flag becomes ambiguous between a property of the threshold rule and a property of a patient/result notification event.
  - action: Split the model into a threshold/range rule concept, such as CriticalValueRange or CriticalRule, and a result-linked notification or critical-result event concept. Keep threshold bounds and rule semantics on the rule concept; put notified status, notifier, recipient, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement/status, escalation, and source-log evidence on the event concept. This split should be done before release because downstream EMR/LIS reconciliation and audit behavior depend on having a stable event authority rather than a boolean attached to a range definition.

- issue-007 (medium): Specimen type modeling materially weakens the ontology because the same specimen-kind concept is split between closed Specimen/Test enums and Assay free strings. This should be carried forward as a medium material issue and resolved by making specimen kind a governed shared code set or entity referenced by Specimen, Test, and Assay.

  - issue statement: Specimen type modeling splits shared specimen semantics between Specimen/Test enums and Assay free strings. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / specimen type modeling Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Specimen.specimen_type` enum values `[blood, urine, tissue, swab]`; `Test.requires_specimen_type` repeats the same enum; `Assay.specimen_kind` is a free string with examples `'WB', 'Serum', 'Urine-random'`. Source finding context: 검체 종류가 enum과 자유 문자열로 갈라져 새 검체 세부 유형이나 외부 LIS 표준 값을 추가할 때 연속성이 깨진다. Source finding context: 검체 분류는 장비, 검사 카탈로그, 결과 해석을 잇는 확장 축이다. 현재처럼 한쪽은 폐쇄 enum이고 다른 한쪽은 문자열이면 새 LIS 검체 코드나 세분 검체가 들어올 때 enum 확장과 문자열 매핑을 동시에 수행해야 하며, 과거 `blood` 같은 넓은 값과 신규 `Serum`/`WB` 같은 세부 값의 연속적 해석이 불명확해진다. Source finding context: 검체 종류를 별도 `SpecimenType`/`SpecimenKind` 권위 엔티티 또는 코드셋으로 승격하고, 넓은 분류와 세부 코드의 계층/alias/버전을 표현한다. `Specimen`, `Test`, `Assay`는 모두 같은 코드셋을 참조하게 하고 외부 LIS 코드 매핑을 별도 속성 또는 관계로 둔다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002 Source finding context: EMR/LIS 통합에서 검체 기반 주문 가능성, 분석 수행, 결과 추적을 확장 가능한 공통 개념으로 유지하는 목적. Source finding context: 새 검체 세부 유형, 외부 LIS 검체 코드, 부서별 검체 명칭을 수용해야 하는 경우. Source finding context: 폐쇄 enum과 자유 문자열이 동시에 존재해 새 값을 넣을 때 기존 enum 수정이 필요하고, 기존 데이터와 신규 세분 코드 사이의 매핑 권위가 없어 통합 규칙의 지속성이 약해진다. Source finding context: 검체 종류 개념이 단일 권위 코드셋 없이 여러 속성 타입으로 중복 표현된다. Source finding context: `Specimen`과 `Test`는 검체 종류를 `[blood, urine, tissue, swab]` 폐쇄 enum으로 제한한다. Source finding context: `Assay`는 같은 검체 축을 자유 문자열로 기록하며 예시는 enum보다 세분화되어 있다. Source finding context: 이 구조는 새 검체 코드 추가 시 enum 구조 변경과 문자열 매핑 규칙 추가를 요구하고, 기존 넓은 값과 신규 세부 값의 연속성을 모델 안에서 보존하지 못한다.
  - affected purpose: Maintain specimen-based orderability, analysis execution, and result tracing as an extensible common concept in EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: EMR/LIS 통합에서 검체 기반 주문 가능성, 분석 수행, 결과 추적을 확장 가능한 공통 개념으로 유지하는 목적.
  - failure condition: New detailed specimen types, external LIS specimen codes, or department-specific specimen names must be introduced. Source finding context: 새 검체 세부 유형, 외부 LIS 검체 코드, 부서별 검체 명칭을 수용해야 하는 경우.
  - impact: Closed enums and free strings require parallel updates and leave no authority for continuity between broad legacy values and detailed new codes. Source finding context: 폐쇄 enum과 자유 문자열이 동시에 존재해 새 값을 넣을 때 기존 enum 수정이 필요하고, 기존 데이터와 신규 세분 코드 사이의 매핑 권위가 없어 통합 규칙의 지속성이 약해진다.
  - root hypothesis: Specimen kind is duplicated across closed enums and free strings without a single governed code-set or mapping authority.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Specimen.specimen_type` enum`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Test.requires_specimen_type` enum`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Assay.specimen_kind` string note examples`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Specimen.specimen_type` enum values `[blood, urine, tissue, swab]`; `Test.requires_specimen_type` repeats the same enum; `Assay.specimen_kind` is a free string with examples `'WB', 'Serum', 'Urine-random'`.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-009`, `finding-009.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Specimen.specimen_type``, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Test.requires_specimen_type``, `finding-009.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Assay.specimen_kind` note`, `finding-009.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Assay.specimen_kind` examples`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-007`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-007.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-012`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: evolution
  - action candidates: follow_up
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to keep specimen-based orderability, analysis execution, and result tracing extensible across EMR/LIS integration. That purpose is weakened because new detailed specimen types, external LIS specimen codes, or department-specific specimen names cannot be introduced through one stable authority: closed enums must be changed while Assay strings require separate mapping rules, leaving broad legacy values such as blood without governed continuity to detailed values such as Serum or WB.
  - root cause: The starting point is that specimen kind is duplicated across different representational forms without a single governed code-set or mapping authority. Because Specimen and Test locally encode specimen type as the same closed enum while Assay records specimen kind as an unconstrained string, no shared concept owns hierarchy, aliases, versions, or external LIS mappings.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported by the modeled split: Specimen.specimen_type and Test.requires_specimen_type restrict the axis to blood, urine, tissue, and swab, while Assay.specimen_kind uses free-string examples that are more detailed, including WB, Serum, and Urine-random. When a new specimen code arrives, the model therefore needs both enum expansion and string mapping, but it has no invariant for equivalence or subsumption between broad and detailed specimen meanings.
  - action: Promote specimen kind to a governed SpecimenType or SpecimenKind code set/entity with hierarchy, aliases, versions, and external LIS-code mappings. Then make Specimen, Test, and Assay reference that same authority so orderability, assay execution, and result tracing share one specimen axis; this can be handled as planned follow-up and should remain aligned with the related Test/Assay catalog-authority work.

- issue-008 (medium): STAT priority is modeled in three parallel ways: `Order.priority=stat`, `Order.is_stat`, and the `StatOrder` subtype. Because no one representation is declared canonical and no invariant binds them, the model can produce divergent STAT meanings when priority policy or EMR/LIS interface mappings change.

  - issue statement: STAT meaning is split across three representations that can diverge under policy or interface changes. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / order priority and STAT extension model Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Order.priority` enum values `[routine, urgent, stat]`; `Order.is_stat` boolean note "priority와 함께 기록"; `StatOrder` is_a `Order` with `stat_reason`. Source finding context: STAT 우선순위가 enum, boolean, subtype으로 중복되어 새 우선순위 정책 추가 시 기존 구조 수정과 해석 충돌이 발생한다. Source finding context: 우선순위 정책은 병원, 부서, 인터페이스 버전에 따라 바뀌기 쉬운 변경 축이다. 현재 모델은 단순 enum 확장만으로도 boolean 및 subtype 의미와 충돌할 수 있고, `stat_reason` 같은 STAT 전용 속성 때문에 새 긴급도 범주를 추가할 때 하위 타입을 계속 늘리거나 기존 `Order` 구조를 수정해야 한다. Source finding context: 우선순위를 단일 권위 개념으로 정하고 `is_stat`는 파생값으로 명시하거나 제거한다. STAT 전용 사유는 `priority_reason` 또는 `OrderPriority` 정책 객체의 속성으로 일반화해 새 우선순위 범주가 기존 `Order` 하위 타입 추가 없이 확장되도록 한다. Source finding context: 새 우선순위 범주, 기관별 STAT 사유 정책, 외부 EMR/LIS 우선순위 코드가 추가되는 경우. Source finding context: 동일 개념의 세 표현이 병렬로 확장되어야 해 권위 충돌과 기존 구조 수정이 필요해지고, 인터페이스 매핑 시 우선순위 의미가 불안정해진다. Source finding context: 우선순위/STAT 개념이 하나의 확장 가능한 정책 권위로 모델링되지 않고 enum, boolean, subtype에 분산되어 있다. Source finding context: `Order`는 `priority` enum에 `stat` 값을 포함한다. Source finding context: 같은 STAT 여부가 `Order.is_stat` boolean으로도 기록된다. Source finding context: `StatOrder` 하위 타입이 다시 STAT 주문을 별도 구조로 정의한다. Source finding context: 따라서 새 우선순위 정책은 단일 값 추가가 아니라 enum, boolean 파생 규칙, subtype 구조의 동시 변경 문제로 번진다.
  - affected purpose: Support consistent order priority and STAT policy changes in the EMR/LIS integration model. Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 응급 정책 변경을 EMR/LIS 통합 모델에서 일관되게 수용하는 목적.
  - failure condition: New priority categories, institutional STAT reason policies, or external EMR/LIS priority codes are added. Source finding context: 새 우선순위 범주, 기관별 STAT 사유 정책, 외부 EMR/LIS 우선순위 코드가 추가되는 경우.
  - impact: Priority meaning becomes unstable because enum, boolean, and subtype must be extended and interpreted together. Source finding context: 동일 개념의 세 표현이 병렬로 확장되어야 해 권위 충돌과 기존 구조 수정이 필요해지고, 인터페이스 매핑 시 우선순위 의미가 불안정해진다.
  - root hypothesis: STAT priority is modeled redundantly as a priority enum value, an is_stat boolean, and a StatOrder subtype without a canonical source or invariant.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Order.priority` enum`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Order.is_stat` note`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `StatOrder is_a Order``, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Order.priority` enum values `[routine, urgent, stat]`; `Order.is_stat` boolean note "priority와 함께 기록"; `StatOrder` is_a `Order` with `stat_reason`.`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-010`, `finding-010.cause-001`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Order.priority``, `finding-010.cause-002`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `Order.is_stat``, `finding-010.cause-003`, `Embedded Materialized Input: `StatOrder``, `finding-010.cause-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-008`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-008.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: evolution
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the purpose of consistently supporting order priority and STAT policy changes in an EMR/LIS integration model. When new priority categories, institution-specific STAT reason policies, or external priority codes are added, consumers must interpret and update an enum value, a boolean, and a subtype together, making priority meaning unstable and increasing the risk of conflicting routing, SLA, alerting, or reporting behavior.
  - root cause: The starting point is the absence of a single priority/STAT authority. STAT is encoded as an enum value, duplicated as a boolean, and reified as a subtype with STAT-specific fields, but the model does not define which surface owns the truth or how the others are derived from it.
  - causal path: The causal path is direct: `Order.priority` includes `stat`; the same STAT fact is also recorded through `Order.is_stat`; then `StatOrder` models STAT orders again as a subtype. This turns priority-policy evolution into coordinated changes across enum semantics, boolean derivation rules, and subtype structure, so even a simple new urgency category or interface code can create inconsistent interpretations.
  - action: Choose one canonical priority/STAT authority before dependent integration design proceeds. Prefer an extensible priority concept or policy object as the source of truth, then remove `is_stat` or mark it as derived, and either remove `StatOrder` or constrain it with explicit invariants. STAT-specific reasons should be generalized into `priority_reason` or priority-policy attributes so new categories do not require new order subtypes or structural edits to `Order`.

- issue-009 (medium): Order.completed is unsafe as a current lifecycle authority because it can be reached when all Results are final, while later allowed Result corrections can invalidate that condition without any modeled Order transition.
  - issue stance agreement: 5/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, logic, structure, evolution, semantics
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: none
  - insufficient evidence lenses: coverage
  - resolution accepted by: 1/1 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: logic
  - remaining disagreement: 0/1 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: logic
  - issue statement: Order.completed can be reached when all results are final, but later result corrections are allowed without an order transition rule. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Order completion rule Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:23, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:71, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:122, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:123 Source finding context: Order.completed is triggered by all Results being final, but later corrected Results are explicitly allowed without a corresponding Order state transition rule. Source finding context: This is a temporal inter-claim conflict if completed is used as the current completion predicate for the order. A completion judgment based on mutable Result.status can be made true and later invalidated by an allowed correction event, but the lifecycle has no state that represents that invalidation. That makes the state model unsafe as an authority for integration behavior that relies on completed meaning all results are final. Source finding context: Define whether Order.completed is a historical transition event or a maintained invariant. If it is maintained, add a correction/reopened/amended state or transition on Result.status=corrected. If it is historical, rename or document completed as release-completed and add a separate current-result-finality predicate. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use as an EMR/LIS integration authority for lifecycle and state interpretation. Source finding context: An Order reaches completed after all Results are final, then a Result is later corrected as allowed by the report correction rule. Source finding context: Integrations can see Order.completed and a corrected/non-final Result at the same time, undermining invalid-input handling and lifecycle precedence for downstream consumers. Source finding context: The model defines order completion against a reversible Result.status value but does not define how order lifecycle responds when that value changes after completion. Source finding context: Order has a completed lifecycle state but no reopened, corrected, or amended order state. Source finding context: Order completion is triggered when all Results are final. Source finding context: Result.status can later become corrected, so the all-final condition can stop holding after completion.
  - affected purpose: Use as an EMR/LIS integration authority for lifecycle and state interpretation.
  - failure condition: An Order reaches completed after all Results are final, then a Result is later corrected. Source finding context: An Order reaches completed after all Results are final, then a Result is later corrected as allowed by the report correction rule.
  - impact: Integrations can observe Order.completed alongside a corrected or non-final Result, undermining lifecycle precedence and invalid-input handling. Source finding context: Integrations can see Order.completed and a corrected/non-final Result at the same time, undermining invalid-input handling and lifecycle precedence for downstream consumers.
  - root hypothesis: The model defines order completion against a reversible Result.status value but does not define how order lifecycle responds when that value changes after completion.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:23`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:71`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:122`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:123`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:23, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:71, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:122, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:123`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-013`, `finding-013.cause-001`, `finding-013.cause-002`, `finding-013.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-002`, `rel-004`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: logic
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the declared EMR/LIS integration purpose because downstream systems may treat Order.completed as authoritative lifecycle state while also seeing a corrected or non-final Result. That creates conflicting current-state signals for lifecycle precedence and invalid-input handling.
  - root cause: The root cause starts at the dependency between Order.completed and mutable Result.status: completion is defined from all Results being final, but Result.status can later change to corrected. Because the model does not define what happens to the Order after that change, the lifecycle state lacks authority over the current order condition.
  - causal path: The causal chain is: Order has a completed state with no reopened, corrected, or amended companion state; completion is triggered when all Results are final; Result.status is allowed to later become corrected. Once that correction occurs, the all-final basis for completion may no longer hold, yet the Order remains completed in the modeled lifecycle.
  - action: Define completed as either a historical release-completion event or a maintained invariant. If maintained, add explicit Order behavior for correction, such as reopened, corrected, or amended transitions; if historical, rename or document completed accordingly and add a separate current-result-finality predicate for integrations that need present-state truth.

Source finding context: 세 요소는 모두 같은 STAT/응급 주문 의미를 가리키지만 서로의 결정 관계가 정의되지 않았습니다. priority=stat, is_stat=true, 인스턴스 타입 StatOrder가 항상 같은 의미인지 불명확해 EMR/LIS 간 우선순위 전달과 라우팅 조건의 권위 필드가 갈라집니다. Source finding context: STAT 의미의 canonical source를 하나로 정하십시오. 예를 들어 priority=stat만 권위로 두고 is_stat과 StatOrder를 제거하거나 파생/뷰로 명시하거나, StatOrder를 유지한다면 priority와 is_stat의 필수 불변식 및 매핑을 정의하십시오. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 STAT 라우팅 의미를 EMR/LIS 통합에서 일관되게 전달하는 목적. Source finding context: 동일 주문에서 priority, is_stat, StatOrder 타입이 서로 다른 STAT 의미를 나타낼 수 있을 때. Source finding context: 통합 구현자가 서로 다른 필드를 기준으로 STAT 여부를 판정하면 긴급 검사 라우팅, SLA, 알림, 대시보드 집계가 불일치할 수 있습니다. Source finding context: STAT이라는 동일 주문 의미를 속성 값, boolean 파생값, 하위 타입으로 중복 선언했다. Source finding context: Order.priority enum에 stat가 포함되어 있다. Source finding context: Order.is_stat가 STAT 주문 여부를 별도 boolean으로 기록한다. Source finding context: StatOrder도 Order 하위 타입으로 응급(STAT) 검사 주문을 의미한다.
  - affected purpose: Consistently transmit order priority and STAT routing meaning in EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 STAT 라우팅 의미를 EMR/LIS 통합에서 일관되게 전달하는 목적.
  - failure condition: The same order can carry conflicting STAT implications across priority, is_stat, and type membership. Source finding context: 동일 주문에서 priority, is_stat, StatOrder 타입이 서로 다른 STAT 의미를 나타낼 수 있을 때.
  - impact: Different consumers may route urgent tests, enforce SLA, alert, or aggregate dashboards using different STAT authorities. Source finding context: 통합 구현자가 서로 다른 필드를 기준으로 STAT 여부를 판정하면 긴급 검사 라우팅, SLA, 알림, 대시보드 집계가 불일치할 수 있습니다.
  - root hypothesis: STAT is declared as the same order meaning in separate semantic surfaces without a defined decision relationship among them.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16-24`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:10-24`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-015`, `finding-015.cause-001`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:16`, `finding-015.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:17`, `finding-015.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:20-24`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-011`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: The declared purpose is to transmit order priority and STAT routing meaning consistently across EMR/LIS integration. If the same order can imply different STAT states through priority, is_stat, and subtype membership, downstream consumers may choose different authorities for urgent routing, SLA handling, alerts, and dashboard aggregation, producing inconsistent operational behavior.
  - root cause: The starting point is that STAT is modeled as the same order meaning in three separate semantic surfaces: an enum value, a boolean flag, and a subtype. Because no decision relationship is declared among them, none of the three is clearly authoritative and the others are not clearly derived or constrained.
  - causal path: The causal path is straightforward: Order.priority includes stat, Order.is_stat separately records STAT order status, and StatOrder also denotes an urgent STAT order. Since these all encode the same concept without a required mapping or invariant, an instance can carry conflicting STAT implications, which exposes the ontology-level inconsistency described by the issue.
  - action: Choose one canonical source for STAT meaning and then either remove the other surfaces, mark them as derived views, or define mandatory invariants that force them to agree. This decision should be made before the next stage because other routing, SLA, alerting, and reporting logic depends on a stable STAT authority.

Source finding context: 검체는 물리적 시료이며 결과 값을 직접 생산하는 행위자나 수행 프로세스가 아닙니다. 결과는 검사/assay 수행에서 생성되고 검체에서 유래합니다. 따라서 produces는 검체의 존재론적 유형을 과도하게 행위자로 만들며, derived_from과 같은 lineage 의미를 다른 동사로 반복해 관계 의미를 흐립니다. Source finding context: Specimen->Result produces를 제거하거나 relation kind를 source_for/provides_material_for처럼 lineage 의미로 바꾸십시오. 결과 생성 주체가 필요하다면 Assay/Test execution 같은 수행 개념을 도입해 Result produced_by 관계를 그 수행 개념에 연결하십시오. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-005 Source finding context: 검체, 검사 수행, 결과 산출의 의미 관계를 EMR/LIS 통합 권위 문서에서 정확히 구분하는 목적. Source finding context: Result는 Test 수행 결과 값으로 정의되어 수행 행위의 산출물임을 암시한다. Source finding context: Result to Specimen derived_from 관계가 이미 검체 유래 의미를 표현한다. Source finding context: Specimen to Result produces 관계가 같은 검체-결과 연결을 생산 관계로 다시 표현한다.
  - affected purpose: Accurately distinguish specimen, test execution, and result production in the EMR/LIS integration authority model. Source finding context: 검체, 검사 수행, 결과 산출의 의미 관계를 EMR/LIS 통합 권위 문서에서 정확히 구분하는 목적.
  - failure condition: An implementer treats Specimen produces Result as the authoritative result-generation relationship. Source finding context: 통합 구현자가 Specimen produces Result를 결과 생성의 권위 관계로 해석할 때.
  - impact: Result creation, material lineage, equipment or department responsibility, and assay execution can be conflated in downstream data flows. Source finding context: 결과의 생성 주체와 유래 물질이 혼동되어 assay 수행, 장비/부서 책임, specimen lineage를 분리해야 하는 운영 모델에서 잘못된 데이터 흐름을 만들 수 있습니다.
  - root hypothesis: The specimen-to-result lineage relationship is named as production, overexpressing a physical specimen as the producer of a result.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-005`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:57-68`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:103-114`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:57-68,103-114`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-018`, `finding-018.cause-001`, `finding-018.cause-002`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:113`, `finding-018.cause-003`, `clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:114`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-012`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-012.structure`, `Decision whether to rename the specimen-result edge to lineage/source semantics or introduce and wire an execution concept such as Assay/Test execution as Result producer.`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-005`, `rel-015`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: follow_up, accept_risk
  - materiality: This weakens the EMR/LIS authority model's purpose of separating specimen identity, test execution, and result production. If implementers treat Specimen produces Result as the authoritative generation relation, downstream flows can conflate material lineage with assay execution, equipment or department responsibility, and result creation.
  - root cause: The starting point is that a source/lineage relationship from specimen to result was named as production. That overexpresses the specimen as an agent or process, even though the specimen is physical source material and not the execution event that creates the result.
  - causal path: The ontology defines Result as the value produced by test performance, which points to an execution concept as the natural producer. It also already states Result derived_from Specimen, covering the specimen-lineage meaning. Adding Specimen produces Result reuses the same specimen-result connection with production semantics, creating conflict and ambiguity between source material and result producer.
  - action: Repair the relation by either renaming/remodeling the specimen-result edge as lineage/source semantics, such as source_for or provides_material_for, or by introducing and wiring an Assay/Test execution concept as the actual producer of Result. This should be coordinated with the shared missing execution/result-production concept context captured for issue-014.

- issue-013 (medium): Patient is referenced by Order.patient_ref but is not represented in the explicit workflow relations graph. This leaves Patient outside the traversable Order-to-Report relation model unless attribute references are formally documented as authoritative relationships.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Patient connectivity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:15-20,31-35,108-119 Source finding context: Patient is only present as an Order attribute target and is absent from the explicit relations graph. Source finding context: The file says it is intended as the EMR/LIS integration concept authority and defines the lab pipeline from Order to Report. If the explicit relations graph is the structural contract, the patient dimension is not traversable from the workflow relations even though orders are issued for patients. Source finding context: Add explicit patient relations, for example Order -> Patient belongs_to and Patient -> Order has_many, or document that attribute refs are the authoritative relationship surface and keep relations consistent with that rule. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: Using the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report. Source finding context: Consumers that rely on the relations section to derive integration paths cannot connect lab orders, specimens, results, or reports to Patient. Source finding context: This weakens trust in the model as an integration authority because a core workflow participant is structurally outside the declared relation graph. Source finding context: The ontology splits structural linkage between attribute refs and the relations list without making Patient an explicit relation participant. Source finding context: Patient is declared as a standalone entity and Order.patient_ref targets Patient. Source finding context: The explicit relations list enumerates workflow edges but contains no edge whose from or to is Patient. Source finding context: A relation-graph consumer therefore has no explicit Patient path in the Order-to-Report workflow.
  - affected purpose: Using the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report.
  - failure condition: Consumers rely on the relations section to derive integration paths across orders, specimens, results, reports, and patients.   - impact: A core workflow participant is structurally outside the declared relation graph, weakening the model as an integration authority. Source finding context: This weakens trust in the model as an integration authority because a core workflow participant is structurally outside the declared relation graph.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology splits structural linkage between attribute refs and the relations list without making Patient an explicit relation participant.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001`, `materialized-input.md:6-8`, `materialized-input.md:15-20`, `materialized-input.md:31-35`, `materialized-input.md:108-119`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:15-20,31-35,108-119`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-019`, `finding-019.cause-001`, `finding-019.cause-002`, `finding-019.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.axiology`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-013`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-013.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: follow_up, accept_risk
  - materiality: The ontology is intended to serve as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration from Order through Report. If consumers derive integration paths from the relations section, they cannot connect orders, specimens, results, or reports to Patient, which weakens trust in the model for a core workflow participant.
  - root cause: The causal chain starts with a split between two structural surfaces: Patient is available through an Order attribute reference, while the explicit relations list is the apparent workflow graph. Because Patient is not made an explicit relation participant and no rule elevates attribute refs as graph edges, consumers have no clear authoritative Patient path.
  - causal path: The evidence path is: Patient is declared as an entity and targeted by Order.patient_ref; the explicit relations list enumerates workflow edges but includes no relation from or to Patient; therefore a relation-graph consumer cannot traverse from the lab workflow to Patient in the Order-to-Report model.
  - action: Resolve the structural authority gap by either adding explicit patient relations, such as Order belongs_to Patient and Patient has_many Order, or documenting that attribute references are the authoritative relationship surface and aligning the relations list with that rule. The decision about which surface is authoritative should come first, because it determines whether the fix is a graph change or a contract/documentation alignment change.

  - materiality: This weakens the declared EMR/LIS authority purpose because orderable catalog items and analyzer-executed items must be integrated. A consumer can follow existing graph edges from Test to Result, but cannot determine which Assay implements the Test or produces the Result, leaving an actionable integration gap between EMR ordering and LIS/analyzer execution.
  - root cause: The starting point is the missing governed orderable-to-executable mapping: Assay was introduced as a separate execution catalog concept, but the explicit relations graph never assigns it an implementation or result-production role. Deliberation narrowed this as a bounded structural manifestation of the broader Test-Assay boundary and mapping problem, not a separate competing root.
  - causal path: The causal chain is that Test and Assay are both present as distinct catalog concepts, notes require new items to be registered in both while consolidation is pending, yet the relations graph connects Test, Specimen, Result, Report, Order, Department, and Staff without any Assay edge. Therefore the analyzer execution concept is outside the result-production path even though it is needed to explain how an orderable Test is executed and yields a Result.
  - action: Close this before the next stage by deciding whether Assay belongs in the authoritative workflow graph. If it does, add the minimal execution relations, such as Test implemented_by Assay and Assay produces Result, aligned with the broader issue-006 Test-Assay mapping work. If Assay is not yet authoritative, explicitly de-scope it from the workflow graph until catalog consolidation is complete, so consumers are not misled by an execution concept with no operational path.

- issue-015 (medium): CriticalValue is materially under-modeled for operational workflow use: it is not explicitly reachable from produced Result instances or notification actors, so relation-based consumers cannot traverse from a lab result to critical-value handling or notification completion.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue connectivity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:62-73,100-106,108-119 Source finding context: The entity is only anchored through test_ref to Test at the attribute level. In the explicit relation graph, the critical-value concept cannot be reached from a produced Result, so the workflow does not structurally specify how a result becomes a critical value or how notification completion ties back to the lab report path. Source finding context: Add explicit edges such as Result -> CriticalValue matches_threshold or triggers, CriticalValue -> Staff notified_by/notified_to if those actors are in scope, and link the notification state to the report/result workflow; otherwise mark CriticalValue as a catalog-only threshold entity and move notification state out of it. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003 Source finding context: Using the ontology as the operational concept authority for lab result workflow and LIS reporting behavior. Source finding context: A consumer using relations cannot traverse from a produced Result to critical-value handling or notification status. Source finding context: CriticalValue combines threshold and notification state but is not represented in the explicit workflow relations. Source finding context: CriticalValue represents immediately notifiable dangerous ranges and includes notified state. Source finding context: CriticalValue handling is therefore not structurally connected to actual Result workflow instances.
  - affected purpose: Using the ontology as the operational concept authority for lab result workflow and LIS reporting behavior.
  - failure condition: A consumer using relations needs to traverse from a produced Result to critical-value handling or notification status. Source finding context: A consumer using relations cannot traverse from a produced Result to critical-value handling or notification status.
  - impact: A safety-relevant workflow concept is detached from the result/report path that would drive operational handling. Source finding context: This leaves a safety-relevant workflow concept structurally detached from the result/report path that would need to drive operational handling.
  - root hypothesis: CriticalValue combines threshold and notification state but is not represented in the explicit workflow relations.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003`, `materialized-input.md:62-73`, `materialized-input.md:100-106`, `materialized-input.md:108-119`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:62-73,100-106,108-119`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-021`, `finding-021.cause-001`, `finding-021.cause-002`, `finding-021.cause-003`, `finding-021.cause-004`, `materialized-input.md:100-119`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.axiology`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-015`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.evolution`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.semantics`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-015.structure`, `Decision whether CriticalValue remains catalog-only with notification state moved to a separate event concept, or is explicitly split/typed with Result and actor relations.`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose because the ontology is meant to act as operational concept authority for lab result workflow and LIS reporting behavior. Critical-value handling is safety-relevant; if it is detached from the Result and report path, the ontology cannot reliably drive or audit the workflow that identifies a critical result and confirms notification.
  - root cause: The starting point is that CriticalValue combines two meanings: a threshold catalog concept and result-specific notification state. Because those meanings are not separated or explicitly typed, the model has no stable relation target for produced Result instances or for notification actors.
  - causal path: Result is modeled as the produced test result with status and verifier fields, while CriticalValue is modeled as immediately notifiable dangerous ranges with notified state. The explicit relation graph connects Result to Report and Specimen, but provides no CriticalValue edge. As a result, CriticalValue handling remains only indirectly attribute-anchored through Test and is not structurally connected to actual Result workflow instances or notification actors.
  - action: Resolve the threshold-versus-event modeling decision before the next stage. Either make CriticalValue catalog-only and move notification state into a separate result-specific notification event, or explicitly split/type the concept so Result-to-critical-handling and actor notification traversal are represented. Simply adding edges to the mixed concept is not enough unless the threshold and notification-event authority distinction is also settled.

### Conditional Consensus
- issue-001 (no-deliberation-needed): Corrected LIS results can coexist with a clinician-facing Report.result_status that still reads finalized or preliminary until nightly synchronization, so the ontology leaves clinicians and EMR/LIS integrations without a determinate trusted status during the correction window.
- issue-002 (no-deliberation-needed): Critical-value notification is materially under-modeled: the ontology uses a bare `notified` boolean where EMR/LIS integration needs an auditable notification event with recipient, notifier, time, channel, acknowledgement, and source authority.
- issue-003 (narrowed): TAT is a material authority gap: it is named as an ontology concept, but the ontology neither governs it as a derived metric nor clearly declares the dashboard formula outside its authority boundary.
- issue-004 (no-deliberation-needed): Specimen lifecycle is materially incomplete because the shared ontology stops at analyzed and does not model or govern post-analysis custody, retention, retrieval, storage, or disposal states/events.
- issue-005 (no-deliberation-needed): CriticalValue currently conflates the definition of a dangerous threshold/range with the state of notifying someone about a patient-specific critical result. This makes the ontology unable to cleanly represent either the threshold rule or the auditable notification event.
- issue-007 (no-deliberation-needed): Specimen type modeling materially weakens the ontology because the same specimen-kind concept is split between closed Specimen/Test enums and Assay free strings. This should be carried forward as a medium material issue and resolved by making specimen kind a governed shared code set or entity referenced by Specimen, Test, and Assay.
- issue-008 (no-deliberation-needed): STAT priority is modeled in three parallel ways: `Order.priority=stat`, `Order.is_stat`, and the `StatOrder` subtype. Because no one representation is declared canonical and no invariant binds them, the model can produce divergent STAT meanings when priority policy or EMR/LIS interface mappings change.
- issue-009 (no-deliberation-needed): Order.completed is unsafe as a current lifecycle authority because it can be reached when all Results are final, while later allowed Result corrections can invalidate that condition without any modeled Order transition.
- issue-011 (no-deliberation-needed): priority=stat, is_stat=true, and StatOrder all represent the same STAT order meaning, but the ontology does not define one canonical authority or an invariant that keeps the three surfaces aligned. This is a material consistency issue that must be carried forward and closed before the next stage.
- issue-012 (narrowed): Specimen produces Result should be retained as a material ontology issue: it frames a physical specimen as the producer of a result, while the model already expresses specimen lineage through Result derived_from Specimen.
- issue-013 (no-deliberation-needed): Patient is referenced by Order.patient_ref but is not represented in the explicit workflow relations graph. This leaves Patient outside the traversable Order-to-Report relation model unless attribute references are formally documented as authoritative relationships.
- issue-014 (narrowed): Assay is materially disconnected from the authoritative workflow graph: the ontology treats Test as the orderable unit and Assay as the analyzer execution unit, but it does not relate Assay to Test or Result, so consumers cannot trace orderable tests through execution to produced results.
- issue-015 (narrowed): CriticalValue is materially under-modeled for operational workflow use: it is not explicitly reachable from produced Result instances or notification actors, so relation-based consumers cannot traverse from a lab result to critical-value handling or notification completion.

### Disagreement
- none

### Axiology-Proposed Additional Perspectives
- issue-001 (high): Corrected LIS results can coexist with a clinician-facing Report.result_status that still reads finalized or preliminary until nightly synchronization, so the ontology leaves clinicians and EMR/LIS integrations without a determinate trusted status during the correction window.
- issue-002 (medium): Critical-value notification is materially under-modeled: the ontology uses a bare `notified` boolean where EMR/LIS integration needs an auditable notification event with recipient, notifier, time, channel, acknowledgement, and source authority.
- issue-003 (medium): TAT is a material authority gap: it is named as an ontology concept, but the ontology neither governs it as a derived metric nor clearly declares the dashboard formula outside its authority boundary.

### Boundary Notes
- Bounded evidence does not state whether downstream tooling is intended to treat attribute refs as graph edges.
- The synthesis is limited to the ontology review artifacts allowed by the unit boundary; downstream integration tooling was not inspected.

### Immediate Actions Required
- issue-001 (high)
  - issue: Corrected LIS results can coexist with a clinician-facing Report.result_status that still reads finalized or preliminary until nightly synchronization, so the ontology leaves clinicians and EMR/LIS integrations without a determinate trusted status during the correction window.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Report.result_status correction handling Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69-83,121-123; .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11 Source finding context: Clinician-trusted report status is allowed to lag corrected LIS results, which misaligns the ontology with its purpose as an EMR/LIS concept authority. Source finding context: The declared review value is suitability as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration, especially operational risk. A concept authority that explicitly lets a clinician-facing authority value remain `finalized` until a nightly batch after a result is corrected accepts a hidden tradeoff against timely clinical truth propagation. That is not merely an implementation detail: the target itself assigns trust to `Report.result_status`, so the delayed propagation weakens the document's purpose as an authority for integration behavior. Source finding context: Make the corrected/amended status a single authoritative state or require event-driven/synchronous propagation from `Result.status=corrected` to `Report.result_status=amended`. If delayed processing is unavoidable, model an explicit interim state such as `amendment_pending`, with timestamp, source result, and EMR-facing trust rules. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority document for EMR/LIS integration, with attention to operational risk. Source finding context: A LIS result changes to `corrected` after report release but before the nightly synchronization batch updates the report status. Source finding context: The EMR-facing status that clinicians are told to trust can remain stale, so downstream integration can present corrected clinical information as finalized during the lag window. Source finding context: The model duplicates result status authority across LIS `Result.status` and clinician-facing `Report.result_status`, then permits asynchronous reconciliation for a trust-critical correction path. Source finding context: `Report.result_status` is described as the authority value shown on the report and trusted by clinicians. Source finding context: The same status meaning is also retained on `Result.status`, which is recorded by LIS. Source finding context: When `Result.status` becomes corrected, `Report.result_status` must become amended, but the synchronization batch runs only once nightly. Source finding context: The requested purpose is to judge whether this ontology is suitable as an EMR/LIS integration concept authority with operational-risk awareness. Source finding context: This is an inter-claim conflict on mixed modality: the attribute note states a maintained equivalence, the state rule states an obligation when corrected, and the same sentence also permits delayed synchronization. As a concept authority document for EMR/LIS integration, this weakens precedence and invalid-input behavior because consumers cannot tell whether Report.result_status is an immediate authority value or an eventually consistent projection. Also specify which value is authoritative during the sync window. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:Result.status and Report.result_status Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:64-78,116-118 Source finding context: Result.status와 Report.result_status가 '동일 정보'라고 정의되지만 서로 다른 상태 어휘와 권위 의미를 사용한다. Source finding context: Report.result_status는 별도 enum [prelim, finalized, amended]를 가지며 임상의가 신뢰하는 권위 값으로 정의된다. Source finding context: 상태 규칙은 Result.status corrected를 Report.result_status amended로 바꾼다고 하여 두 어휘 사이의 암묵 매핑에 의존한다. Source finding context: LIS Result.status와 EMR/임상 Report.result_status가 서로 다른 enum 이름과 의미를 사용하면서도 동일 정보 및 권위 값으로 선언될 때. Source finding context: 새 결과 상태나 보고 상태, 외부 시스템 상태 코드, 동기화 방식 변경이 추가되는 경우.
