- issue-002 (medium)
  - issue: Critical-value notification is materially under-modeled: the ontology uses a bare `notified` boolean where EMR/LIS integration needs an auditable notification event with recipient, notifier, time, channel, acknowledgement, and source authority.
  - target: Critical-value notification is represented as a boolean while recipient, time, acknowledgement, and authority evidence remain outside the ontology. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification model Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:100-106; .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11 Source finding context: Critical-value notification is reduced to a boolean while the actual recipient and time authority are external, weakening the ontology's operational-risk purpose. Source finding context: For an EMR/LIS integration concept authority, critical-value notification is a stakeholder- and safety-critical workflow, not a peripheral annotation. A boolean cannot represent who was notified, when, by whom, by which channel, or whether acknowledgement closed the loop. By pushing the meaningful notification evidence into an unmodeled ledger, the ontology accepts an implicit tradeoff against traceability and integration clarity without justifying it. Source finding context: Promote critical-value notification into an explicit concept such as `CriticalValueNotification` with result/test reference, recipient, notifier, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement status, and source authority. Relate it to `CriticalValue`, `Result`, `Staff`, and the EMR/LIS release workflow. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use the ontology as the concept authority for EMR/LIS integration while identifying operational risks in entities, relations, and states. Source finding context: A critical value is reported or audited and the integration needs to determine notification timing, recipient, acknowledgement, or authoritative source from the ontology. Source finding context: The ontology cannot support the clinically important closed-loop notification semantics it gestures at, so downstream systems may treat a bare boolean as sufficient evidence of completed risk handling. Source finding context: The model treats critical-value communication evidence as external operational bookkeeping instead of a first-class integration concept. Source finding context: `CriticalValue` represents immediately notifiable dangerous result ranges. Source finding context: The only notification attribute is `notified: boolean`; time and recipient are said to exist in a phone log ledger outside the modeled ontology. Source finding context: The review purpose is suitability as an EMR/LIS integration authority with operational-risk focus.
  - failure condition: A critical value is reported or audited and the integration needs notification timing, recipient, acknowledgement, or authoritative source semantics. Source finding context: A critical value is reported or audited and the integration needs to determine notification timing, recipient, acknowledgement, or authoritative source from the ontology.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - rationale: Promote critical-value notification into an explicit event or entity, such as `CriticalValueNotification`, linked to `CriticalValue`, `Result`, `Staff`, and the EMR/LIS release workflow. It should carry recipient, notifier, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement status, and source authority so systems can distinguish a dangerous result threshold from the communication event that closes or audits the clinical risk loop.
  - remediation: Promote critical-value notification into an explicit event or entity, such as `CriticalValueNotification`, linked to `CriticalValue`, `Result`, `Staff`, and the EMR/LIS release workflow. It should carry recipient, notifier, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement status, and source authority so systems can distinguish a dangerous result threshold from the communication event that closes or audits the clinical risk loop.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-003 (medium)
  - issue: TAT is a material authority gap: it is named as an ontology concept, but the ontology neither governs it as a derived metric nor clearly declares the dashboard formula outside its authority boundary.
  - target: TAT is mentioned as an ontology concept but its calculation authority is left to an external dashboard formula without governed metric semantics. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / deferred local metric and catalog authorities Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:46-60,126-128; .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-11 Source finding context: The ontology knowingly leaves catalog and TAT authority split across local teams, creating purpose drift for a document intended to be the integration concept authority. Source finding context: The requested purpose is a concept authority for EMR/LIS integration. Leaving dual catalog registration and a separate dashboard-owned TAT formula as accepted current behavior privileges local operational convenience over canonical concept authority. The problem is not that future cleanup is planned; it is that the current authority document records the split without a governing precedence rule, drift-detection obligation, or ownership boundary. Source finding context: Add explicit authority and precedence rules: define whether `Test` or `Assay` owns orderable versus executable catalog identity, how dual registration is reconciled, and which source owns TAT semantics. If the dashboard formula remains separate, model it as a derived projection with required conformance to the ontology's canonical TAT definition. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: Use the ontology as a concept authority for EMR/LIS integration. Source finding context: A new test/assay or TAT-consuming integration is implemented while catalog identity or TAT semantics differ between the ontology and local dashboard/catalog maintenance practices. Source finding context: Downstream consumers can implement conflicting meanings while still appearing to follow the authority document, reducing trust in the ontology as the integration source of truth. Source finding context: The ontology accepts unresolved local authority splits as current practice without encoding precedence, ownership, or reconciliation duties. Source finding context: `Test` is the orderable catalog unit, while `Assay` is the analyzer execution unit with overlapping department and specimen-kind semantics. Source finding context: The notes require new items to be registered in both places until future catalog cleanup. Source finding context: The notes define TAT from collection to release, but also say the dashboard team maintains its own calculation formula. Source finding context: The document is being reviewed for suitability as an EMR/LIS integration concept authority. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / TAT calculation authority Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:32-39, clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:70-79, clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:121-123 Source finding context: Turnaround-time coverage is reduced to an external dashboard formula without a governed metric concept or authority. Source finding context: TAT is an operational performance value spanning specimen collection and report release. Because the artifact positions itself as the EMR/LIS authority model, leaving a cross-system metric to an external team-owned formula creates a coverage gap for a time-dependent value that consumers are likely to compare or report. Source finding context: Model TAT as a governed derived metric with source timestamps, calculation owner, inclusion/exclusion rules, effective versioning, and behavior for amended/corrected reports; alternatively declare the dashboard metric explicitly out of scope with a canonical integration boundary. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: Use as a concept authority for EMR/LIS integration, including operational values derived across workflow timestamps. Source finding context: When EMR, LIS, or dashboard consumers need the canonical TAT definition or need to reproduce historical TAT after formula changes or report amendments, the ontology points to an external team-owned calculation rather than a governed concept. Source finding context: A key operational metric can diverge across consumers and cannot be reconstructed from the ontology's own authority model beyond the two raw timestamps. Source finding context: The ontology delegates TAT calculation authority to a dashboard-maintained formula instead of modeling TAT as a governed derived concept. Source finding context: The model includes collected_at on Specimen and released_at on Report. Source finding context: The notes define TAT using those timestamps but say the dashboard team maintains its own calculation formula. Source finding context: No ontology concept captures TAT formula ownership, version/effective period, exclusions, or recalculation behavior.
  - failure condition: EMR, LIS, or dashboard consumers need a canonical or reproducible TAT definition after formula changes or report amendments. Source finding context: A new test/assay or TAT-consuming integration is implemented while catalog identity or TAT semantics differ between the ontology and local dashboard/catalog maintenance practices. Source finding context: When EMR, LIS, or dashboard consumers need the canonical TAT definition or need to reproduce historical TAT after formula changes or report amendments, the ontology points to an external team-owned calculation rather than a governed concept.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, accept_risk
  - rationale: Before the next stage, decide whether TAT is inside or outside ontology authority. If inside, model it as a governed derived metric with source timestamps, owner, version/effective rules, exclusions, conformance requirements, and amended/corrected report behavior. If outside, explicitly declare the dashboard metric out of scope and define the integration boundary so consumers do not mistake it for canonical ontology semantics.
  - remediation: Before the next stage, decide whether TAT is inside or outside ontology authority. If inside, model it as a governed derived metric with source timestamps, owner, version/effective rules, exclusions, conformance requirements, and amended/corrected report behavior. If outside, explicitly declare the dashboard metric out of scope and define the integration boundary so consumers do not mistake it for canonical ontology semantics.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / Specimen lifecycle Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:32-39, clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:116-119 Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle omits the post-analysis custody, retention, and disposal domain segment. Source finding context: Specimen lifecycle terminates at analyzed. Source finding context: The ontology nevertheless acknowledges storage/disposal after analyzed as an operational process. Source finding context: Because no shared post-analysis lifecycle concepts are defined, EMR/LIS integrations lack a canonical representation for specimen custody after analysis.

  - remediation: Add explicit post-analysis specimen lifecycle concepts such as retained/stored, retrieved, disposed, and retention/disposal events, including timestamp, responsible department or staff, location, and policy basis. If the detailed rules must remain departmental, add a governed extension point that is still visible in the authority model so downstream integrations have a canonical mapping target.

  - issue: CriticalValue currently conflates the definition of a dangerous threshold/range with the state of notifying someone about a patient-specific critical result. This makes the ontology unable to cleanly represent either the threshold rule or the auditable notification event.
  - target: CriticalValue mixes dangerous range thresholds with notification state, so notification evidence and threshold rules are not cleanly separated. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue notification coverage Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:95-101 Source finding context: Critical-value notification is represented only as a boolean and excludes notification evidence concepts. Source finding context: Critical-value communication is a controlled action in the target domain because the entity is defined as requiring immediate notification. A boolean cannot reconstruct who notified whom, when, through what channel, and with what acknowledgement, so the authority model does not cover the audit dimension needed for operational trust. Source finding context: Replace or supplement notified with a CriticalValueNotification event/entity containing critical_value/result reference, notifier, recipient, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement/status, escalation fields, and evidence/source-log linkage. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use as an EMR/LIS integration concept authority for operationally risky lab workflow states and controlled actions. Source finding context: When a critical result requires proof of notification or reconciliation between LIS, EMR, and a phone log, the model exposes only a true/false flag and no auditable event structure. Source finding context: Systems can disagree or overwrite the notification flag without preserving the evidence needed to establish clinical communication history. Source finding context: The ontology keeps critical-value notification evidence outside the modeled concept set and collapses notification into a boolean. Source finding context: CriticalValue is defined as a result range requiring immediate notification. Source finding context: The only in-model notification attribute is notified: boolean, with time and recipient delegated to a phone log. Source finding context: The model therefore lacks the actor/time/recipient/evidence concepts needed to cover the controlled notification action inside the EMR/LIS authority model. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:CriticalValue Source finding context: CriticalValue가 위험 결과 값 범위와 통보 완료 상태를 한 개념에 섞어 threshold rule과 notification event의 존재론적 유형을 혼합한다. Source finding context: 위험 결과 값 범위는 검사별 판정 기준 또는 rule이고, 통보 여부는 특정 환자 결과에 대한 사건 상태입니다. 이 둘이 하나의 entity에 있으면 '이 범위가 통보되었는가'처럼 범위 자체의 상태로 읽히며, 환자별 critical result occurrence와 기준 정의가 의미적으로 뒤섞입니다. Source finding context: CriticalValueRange 또는 CriticalRule을 기준 개념으로 두고 lower/upper bound만 보유하게 하십시오. 환자 결과에 대한 통보는 CriticalNotification 또는 CriticalResultEvent처럼 Result에 연결된 별도 사건/상태 개념으로 분리하고 notified, notified_at, recipient 등을 그곳에 두십시오. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: 위험 결과 기준과 통보 상태를 EMR/LIS 통합에서 정확히 해석하게 하는 목적. Source finding context: 검사별 위험 범위 정의에 환자별 통보 완료 여부를 저장하거나 해석할 때. Source finding context: 한 range 레코드의 notified 값이 특정 결과 사건인지 기준 자체인지 불명확해 critical value 통보 추적, 재통보, 감사 기록의 의미가 손상될 수 있습니다. Source finding context: 같은 개념 안에 lower_bound/upper_bound와 notified boolean이 함께 존재한다. Source finding context: notified 주석은 통보 시각과 수신자가 별도 전화 기록 대장에 있다고 하여 실제 통보 사건 정보가 range 정의 바깥에 있음을 드러낸다.
  - failure condition: A critical result requires proof of notification, threshold interpretation, or reconciliation between LIS, EMR, and a phone log. Source finding context: When a critical result requires proof of notification or reconciliation between LIS, EMR, and a phone log, the model exposes only a true/false flag and no auditable event structure. Source finding context: 검사별 위험 범위 정의에 환자별 통보 완료 여부를 저장하거나 해석할 때.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Split the model into a threshold/range rule concept, such as CriticalValueRange or CriticalRule, and a result-linked notification or critical-result event concept. Keep threshold bounds and rule semantics on the rule concept; put notified status, notifier, recipient, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement/status, escalation, and source-log evidence on the event concept. This split should be done before release because downstream EMR/LIS reconciliation and audit behavior depend on having a stable event authority rather than a boolean attached to a range definition.
  - remediation: Split the model into a threshold/range rule concept, such as CriticalValueRange or CriticalRule, and a result-linked notification or critical-result event concept. Keep threshold bounds and rule semantics on the rule concept; put notified status, notifier, recipient, notified_at, channel, acknowledgement/status, escalation, and source-log evidence on the event concept. This split should be done before release because downstream EMR/LIS reconciliation and audit behavior depend on having a stable event authority rather than a boolean attached to a range definition.

Source finding context: 검체 종류를 별도 `SpecimenType`/`SpecimenKind` 권위 엔티티 또는 코드셋으로 승격하고, 넓은 분류와 세부 코드의 계층/alias/버전을 표현한다. `Specimen`, `Test`, `Assay`는 모두 같은 코드셋을 참조하게 하고 외부 LIS 코드 매핑을 별도 속성 또는 관계로 둔다. - issue-008 (medium)
  - issue: STAT priority is modeled in three parallel ways: `Order.priority=stat`, `Order.is_stat`, and the `StatOrder` subtype. Because no one representation is declared canonical and no invariant binds them, the model can produce divergent STAT meanings when priority policy or EMR/LIS interface mappings change.
  - target: STAT meaning is split across three representations that can diverge under policy or interface changes. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / order priority and STAT extension model Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Order.priority` enum values `[routine, urgent, stat]`; `Order.is_stat` boolean note "priority와 함께 기록"; `StatOrder` is_a `Order` with `stat_reason`. Source finding context: STAT 우선순위가 enum, boolean, subtype으로 중복되어 새 우선순위 정책 추가 시 기존 구조 수정과 해석 충돌이 발생한다. Source finding context: 우선순위 정책은 병원, 부서, 인터페이스 버전에 따라 바뀌기 쉬운 변경 축이다. 현재 모델은 단순 enum 확장만으로도 boolean 및 subtype 의미와 충돌할 수 있고, `stat_reason` 같은 STAT 전용 속성 때문에 새 긴급도 범주를 추가할 때 하위 타입을 계속 늘리거나 기존 `Order` 구조를 수정해야 한다. Source finding context: 우선순위를 단일 권위 개념으로 정하고 `is_stat`는 파생값으로 명시하거나 제거한다. STAT 전용 사유는 `priority_reason` 또는 `OrderPriority` 정책 객체의 속성으로 일반화해 새 우선순위 범주가 기존 `Order` 하위 타입 추가 없이 확장되도록 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003 Source finding context: 주문 우선순위와 응급 정책 변경을 EMR/LIS 통합 모델에서 일관되게 수용하는 목적. Source finding context: 새 우선순위 범주, 기관별 STAT 사유 정책, 외부 EMR/LIS 우선순위 코드가 추가되는 경우. Source finding context: 동일 개념의 세 표현이 병렬로 확장되어야 해 권위 충돌과 기존 구조 수정이 필요해지고, 인터페이스 매핑 시 우선순위 의미가 불안정해진다. Source finding context: 우선순위/STAT 개념이 하나의 확장 가능한 정책 권위로 모델링되지 않고 enum, boolean, subtype에 분산되어 있다. Source finding context: `Order`는 `priority` enum에 `stat` 값을 포함한다. Source finding context: 같은 STAT 여부가 `Order.is_stat` boolean으로도 기록된다. Source finding context: `StatOrder` 하위 타입이 다시 STAT 주문을 별도 구조로 정의한다. Source finding context: 따라서 새 우선순위 정책은 단일 값 추가가 아니라 enum, boolean 파생 규칙, subtype 구조의 동시 변경 문제로 번진다.

Prefer an extensible priority concept or policy object as the source of truth, then remove `is_stat` or mark it as derived, and either remove `StatOrder` or constrain it with explicit invariants. - issue-009 (medium)
  - issue: Order.completed is unsafe as a current lifecycle authority because it can be reached when all Results are final, while later allowed Result corrections can invalidate that condition without any modeled Order transition.
  - target: Order.completed can be reached when all results are final, but later result corrections are allowed without an order transition rule. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml Order completion rule Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:23, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:71, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:122, .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:123 Source finding context: Order.completed is triggered by all Results being final, but later corrected Results are explicitly allowed without a corresponding Order state transition rule. Source finding context: This is a temporal inter-claim conflict if completed is used as the current completion predicate for the order. A completion judgment based on mutable Result.status can be made true and later invalidated by an allowed correction event, but the lifecycle has no state that represents that invalidation. That makes the state model unsafe as an authority for integration behavior that relies on completed meaning all results are final. Source finding context: Define whether Order.completed is a historical transition event or a maintained invariant. If it is maintained, add a correction/reopened/amended state or transition on Result.status=corrected. If it is historical, rename or document completed as release-completed and add a separate current-result-finality predicate. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-002 Source finding context: Use as an EMR/LIS integration authority for lifecycle and state interpretation. Source finding context: An Order reaches completed after all Results are final, then a Result is later corrected as allowed by the report correction rule. Source finding context: Integrations can see Order.completed and a corrected/non-final Result at the same time, undermining invalid-input handling and lifecycle precedence for downstream consumers. Source finding context: The model defines order completion against a reversible Result.status value but does not define how order lifecycle responds when that value changes after completion. Source finding context: Order has a completed lifecycle state but no reopened, corrected, or amended order state. Source finding context: Order completion is triggered when all Results are final. Source finding context: Result.status can later become corrected, so the all-final condition can stop holding after completion.
  - failure condition: An Order reaches completed after all Results are final, then a Result is later corrected. Source finding context: An Order reaches completed after all Results are final, then a Result is later corrected as allowed by the report correction rule.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - rationale: Define completed as either a historical release-completion event or a maintained invariant. If maintained, add explicit Order behavior for correction, such as reopened, corrected, or amended transitions; if historical, rename or document completed accordingly and add a separate current-result-finality predicate for integrations that need present-state truth.
  - remediation: Define completed as either a historical release-completion event or a maintained invariant. If maintained, add explicit Order behavior for correction, such as reopened, corrected, or amended transitions; if historical, rename or document completed accordingly and add a separate current-result-finality predicate for integrations that need present-state truth.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing

  - issue: Specimen produces Result should be retained as a material ontology issue: it frames a physical specimen as the producer of a result, while the model already expresses specimen lineage through Result derived_from Specimen.

Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:Specimen-to-Result relations Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml:57-68,103-114 Source finding context: Specimen이 Result를 produces한다고 한 관계 이름이 검체의 의미와 맞지 않고, 이미 derived_from으로 표현된 의미와 충돌한다. - issue-015 (medium)
  - issue: CriticalValue is materially under-modeled for operational workflow use: it is not explicitly reachable from produced Result instances or notification actors, so relation-based consumers cannot traverse from a lab result to critical-value handling or notification completion.
  - target: CriticalValue is not structurally connected to actual Result instances or notification actors. Source finding context: clinical-lab-ontology.yaml / CriticalValue connectivity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-4d4a75e9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:62-73,100-106,108-119 Source finding context: The entity is only anchored through test_ref to Test at the attribute level.   - candidates: fix_before_release, follow_up
  - rationale: Resolve the threshold-versus-event modeling decision before the next stage. Either make CriticalValue catalog-only and move notification state into a separate result-specific notification event, or explicitly split/type the concept so Result-to-critical-handling and actor notification traversal are represented. Simply adding edges to the mixed concept is not enough unless the threshold and notification-event authority distinction is also settled.
  - remediation: Resolve the threshold-versus-event modeling decision before the next stage. Either make CriticalValue catalog-only and move notification state into a separate result-specific notification event, or explicitly split/type the concept so Result-to-critical-handling and actor notification traversal are represented.
